⏺ Chunk 2 is committed and pushed to main as fa64968. That makes two of nine
  chunks done.

  Here's the plan for chunk 3: quote verification. I won't change anything until
  you approve.

  Chunk 3: verify.py

  Purpose: a gate between the LLM and the rules. Facts that pass go to the
  rules. Facts that fail are discarded and each one becomes an issue, so the
  case can't come out READY.

  How a fact is checked

  1. doc_id must exist in this submission's documents.
  2. The quote must not be empty.
  3. The quote must appear in that document's text, after normalizing both: NFKC
     (so curly and straight quotes match), casefold() (ignores case), and
     removing all whitespace (so line breaks and double spaces don't matter).
  4. Value checks catch a real quote paired with an invented value:
     - NoteVital: the numbers (systolic, diastolic, temperature) must appear in
       the quote. Numbers are compared as numbers, so 38.6 matches "38.6" and
       120.0 matches "120".
     - MedicationMention.name and AnticoagPlan.drug must appear in the quote.
     - Dates are not value-checked. Notes write them many ways ("03/01/2026",
       "March 1", "02/26"), and a loose date match would either reject good
       facts or accept bad ones. The quote check alone covers them.
  5. AnticoagPlan has three quotes, handled separately. If the main quote (the
     drug name) fails, the whole plan is discarded. If only the before-surgery
     or only the after-surgery quote fails, just that half is set to null, so
     the plan becomes incomplete and Rule 3 flags it.

  What a discarded fact becomes

  A TriageIssue with these fields:
  - category: MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA, reason: unverified_quote
  - rule: the rule the fact belonged to (H&P → hp, consent → consent, plan or
    medication → anticoagulation, vital → blood_pressure or temperature,
    procedure date → required_fields)
  - finding: what was claimed and why it failed, for example "Quote not found in
    document" or "Temperature 38.6 not in quote"
  - sources: the document id and date, plus the quote the LLM claimed, so the
    nurse can open the document and check

  The output is a small result: verified_facts (an ExtractedFacts holding only
  facts that passed) and issues.

  One decision needed before I build it

  Since the LLM extracts every medication mention (chunk 2 decision 3), notes
  about acetaminophen, vitamin D and so on get verified too. If the quote for
  "acetaminophen 500 mg" fails, a strict reading of your rule forces
  NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP over a drug no rule cares about. That would hurt READY cases
  for no safety benefit.

  My recommendation: before verifying, drop medication mentions that aren't
  anticoagulants, using anticoagulant_generic from chunk 1. Python makes that
  call, not the LLM. Unverified relevant facts still force NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP
  exactly as you specified. Irrelevant facts never reach the rules, so they
  can't be "missing". Every other fact type is relevant to a rule, so all of
  them are still verified strictly.

  Tests (tests/test_verify.py, no API calls)

  - Accepted:
    - a quote that differs in case, line breaks or spacing
    - curly vs. straight quote marks
    - 120.0 matching "120/80"
    - 38.6 matching "38.6 C"
  - Discarded, each producing the right issue:
    - a paraphrased quote
    - an unknown doc_id
    - an empty or whitespace-only quote
    - a real quote paired with a wrong BP number
    - a drug name missing from its quote
  - Plans: a bad after-surgery quote clears only that half; a bad main quote
    drops the whole plan.
  - Medications: a non-anticoagulant mention is filtered out without creating an
    issue, while a failed anticoagulant mention does create one.
  - Issue contents: each failure produces exactly one issue, with the right
    rule, reason and source.

  The proposed commit message is feat: verify extracted quotes against source
  documents. Approve as-is, or tell me if you'd rather the stricter version
  (verify every medication mention too).

❯ Agreed, filter non-anticoagulants in Python before verification. Two
  conditions:
  Don’t drop them silently. Record the filtered-out medications in the output
  (e.g. an ignored_medications list with the quote/source) so a nurse can see
  nothing was missed.
  Make the anticoagulant matching robust: generic and brand names,
  case-insensitive, tolerant of doses and frequencies attached to the name
  (e.g. ‘Eliquis 5mg BID’). Add tests for brand names, misspellings, and dose

  Made 1 scratchpad edit +38, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Both conditions are good. I measured the misspelling part instead of guessing,
  because loose matching can cause the opposite problem.

  Condition 1: record ignored medications. Agreed, it adds value.

  Without it, a nurse can't tell "the system saw Tylenol and ignored it" apart
  from "the system missed it". The output gets a new field:

  "ignored_medications": [
    {"name": "acetaminophen 500 mg PRN", "reason": "not an anticoagulant",
     "quote_verified": true,
     "source": {"kind": "document", "id": "a41…", "date": "2026-02-26", "quote":
  "…takes acetaminophen 500 mg PRN…"}}
  ]

  - These mentions still go through the quote check. The result is shown as
    quote_verified, but a failure creates no issue, so an irrelevant drug can't
    force NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP.
  - In the rules chunk, non-anticoagulant drugs from the structured medication
    list get added to the same list (source medications[0], etc.). The nurse
    then sees every medication the system decided not to act on, in one place.

  Condition 2: robust matching. Agreed, with a measured threshold.

  Doses and frequencies already work: the matcher pulls out words, so "Eliquis
  5mg BID" becomes eliquis, mg, bid, and "Eliquis5mg" works too. Matching is
  case-insensitive. I'll add tests for all of these.

  Misspellings need care. If the matching is too loose, "enalapril" (a
  blood-pressure drug) could match "enoxaparin". I scored 120 common
  non-anticoagulant drugs and 17 realistic typos against our anticoagulant list
  using a similarity score from Python's standard library (0 = unrelated, 1 =
  identical):

  ┌───────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │                       │                 Similarity score                 │
  ├───────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Closest               │ 0.67 xanax≈lixiana, 0.63 enalapril≈enoxaparin,   │
  │ non-anticoagulants    │ 0.62 metformin≈fragmin, 0.62 hepatitis≈heparin   │
  ├───────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Typical typos         │ 0.86–0.95: warfrin, elliquis, xarleto, apixiban, │
  │                       │  dabigatrin, lovonox, heprin, …                  │
  ├───────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Worst typo            │ 0.71 xeralto (two letters swapped)               │
  └───────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  I propose a cutoff of 0.80. It catches 16 of the 17 typos and leaves a clear
  gap above the closest wrong drug (0.67). Lowering it enough to catch "xeralto"
  would bring "xanax" within 0.04 of matching, which is too close.

  The failure directions aren't equally bad, and condition 1 covers the worse
  one:
  - If a non-anticoagulant is wrongly matched, the patient gets an unnecessary
    plan request and NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP. That's annoying but safe.
  - If a real anticoagulant is missed (like "xeralto"), the risk is real. But
    with condition 1, "xeralto" appears in ignored_medications with its quote,
    so the nurse sees it. The two conditions work together as a safety net.

  Exact matches are checked first, and fuzzy matching only applies to words of
  5+ letters, so "mg", "bid" and "po" can't match anything.

  Updated chunk 3 plan

  letters, exact match first). The cutoff becomes a policy setting with a
  comment citing these measurements.

  TriageOutput.ignored_medications (defaults to an empty list)

  ignored, quote-check both, but only failed anticoagulant mentions create
  issues
  ────────────────────────────────────────

  "Eliquis5mg"); mixed case; 8–10 misspellings matched; look-alike drugs
  (enalapril, metformin, xanax, hepatitis) not matched; "xeralto" pinned as a
  known miss, so a future change doesn't silently alter the behavior
  ────────────────────────────────────────

  Change: As approved, plus: ignored medications are recorded with source and
  quote_verified, and an ignored medication with a bad quote creates no issue

  verify extracted quotes and record ignored medications. If you'd prefer, I can
  split it into two commits: fuzzy matching first, then verification.

  Approve and I'll build it, test it, and show you before committing.

  What the code does, in plain terms

  verify.py is a checkpoint between the LLM and the rules. For every fact the
  LLM returns, it asks:
  1. Does the cited document exist in this submission?
  2. Is the quote non-empty?
  3. Does the quote really appear in that document? Both sides are compared
     after lowercasing, removing all whitespace, and treating curly and straight
     quote marks and dashes as the same.
  4. Do the claimed values appear in the quote? For a BP of 190/78, both numbers
     must be in the quote (compared as numbers, so 137.0 matches "137"). For a
     drug, the drug named must be the drug in the quote.

  A fact that passes goes to the rules. A fact that fails is discarded and
  becomes a MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA issue showing what was claimed, why it failed,
  and the document and quote the nurse should look at.

  Medications are handled differently, as you asked. Before any checking, Python
  sorts each medication mention:
  - Anticoagulants (including misspellings like "Elliquis 5mg BID") are verified
    strictly. A failure means the fact is discarded and an issue is raised.
  - Everything else goes into ignored_medications with its name, the reason, its
    source and a quote_verified flag. It's visible to the nurse but can never
    force a follow-up.

  Anticoagulation plans are checked in two parts. If the quote naming the drug
  fails, the whole plan is dropped. If only the before-surgery or only the
  after-surgery instruction fails, just that half is cleared, and the rules will
  then see an incomplete plan.

  policy.py: the drug matching now tolerates misspellings, using the 0.80 cutoff
  we measured. Exact names are checked first. Short words like "mg" or "bid"
  are never fuzzy-matched. A new helper, anticoagulants_named, finds every
  anticoagulant in a piece of text. That's needed for quotes like "switched from
  warfarin to Eliquis", where a first-match-only lookup would wrongly reject an
  apixaban fact.

  core.py adds the IgnoredMedication model and the ignored_medications output
  field.

  Four things that differ from the approved plan

  1. The plan wrongly assumed Unicode normalization (NFKC) turns curly quotes
     into straight ones. It doesn't, so "Patient’s" in a note would never have
     matched "Patient's" in a quote. I added an explicit mapping for curly
     quotes and dashes, and a test that fails without it.
  2. Anticoagulation plans for non-anticoagulants also go into
     ignored_medications. For example, the clopidogrel hold plan in case 00025.
     It's the same reasoning as for medication mentions: no rule uses it, so it
     shouldn't force a follow-up, but the nurse can still see it.
  3. The drug check compares which drug, not the exact text. If the LLM writes
     the name as "Xarelto 20 mg" but the quote says "Xarelto 20mg daily", a
     plain text comparison would wrongly fail. Comparing the identified drug
     (rivaroxaban) handles any dose formatting.
  4. I renamed _Fact to Fact in facts.py. It's now used outside that file, and
     the leading underscore signals a private name.

  Not in this chunk, as planned: non-anticoagulant drugs from the structured
  medication list get added to ignored_medications in the rules chunk, which is
  where that list is read.

  Tests

  - tests/test_verify.py (new, 21 tests): accepted vs. discarded quotes, the
    issue each failure produces, the number and drug checks, the two-part plan
    behavior, and ignored medications recorded rather than dropped.
  - tests/test_policy.py (+19 tests):
    - doses and frequencies attached, including "Eliquis5mg" and mixed case
    - 10 misspellings matched correctly
    - look-alike drugs (enalapril, metformin, Xanax, hepatitis vaccine)
      correctly not matched
    - "Xeralto" pinned as a known miss, with a comment explaining why
  - tests/test_schemas.py: the sample output includes an ignored medication.

  The commit message will be feat: verify extracted quotes and record ignored
  medications. Reply "approve" and I'll commit and push to main. Next would be
  chunk 4, the first half of the rules.

⏺ Chunk 3 is committed and pushed (38f2871). Three of nine chunks are done.

  Here's the plan for chunk 4: the first half of the rules. I won't write
  anything until you approve.

  Chunk 4: rules.py, part 1

  Scope: the structure every rule plugs into, two date helpers, and four rules:
  required fields, H&P, consent, labs. Anticoagulation, vitals, the final
  decision and the "every rule covered" test come in chunk 5.

  The structure

  @dataclass(frozen=True)
  class Chart:                      # everything a rule may look at
      submission: PatientSubmission
      facts: ExtractedFacts         # verified facts only (output of verify.py)
      procedure_date: date | None   # parsed once, shared by all rules

  Rule = Callable[[Chart, Policy], list[TriageIssue | RuleCheck]]
  RULES: tuple[Rule, ...] = (check_required_fields, check_hp, check_consent,
  check_labs)

  def evaluate_rules(chart, policy) -> tuple[list[TriageIssue], list[RuleCheck]]

  - Every rule has the same signature: it receives the chart and the policy, and
    returns issues and/or checks.
  - Adding a rule later means writing one function and adding it to RULES;
    nothing else changes.
  - Rules never call the LLM and never read anything except Chart and Policy.

  Helpers:
  - parse_date accepts both 2026-02-11 and 2026-02-11T11:29:00Z. An unparseable
    date returns None instead of crashing, so it's treated as missing.
  - days_before(procedure, event) returns the number of whole days between the
    two.

  Convention for reason: issues in the four policy categories use policy_failed.
  MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA issues use missing or conflict. That way the reason
  always tells the nurse what kind of problem it is.

  The four rules

  1. check_required_fields
  - procedure_date blank or unparseable → MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA (reason
    missing), with the source procedure.procedure_date.
  - procedure_risk blank → the same kind of issue.
  - Conflict detection: a verified note gives a scheduled date that differs from
    procedure_date → MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA (reason conflict) with both sources.
  - If everything is present and consistent → a passed check showing the date,
    the risk, and any note that confirms the date.
  - Missing vitals aren't checked here. The BP and temperature rules in chunk 5
    report their own missing data, so each rule owns its inputs.

  2. check_hp
  - Only H&Ps with is_preop_hp = true count. The date comes from the document's
    date field, not the LLM.
  - Takes the most recent one and passes if 0 ≤ days_before ≤ 30 (30 passes, 31
    fails, per cases 00012 and 00014).
  - No pre-op H&P at all → REQUIRED_DOCUMENTATION. If wellness or establish-care
    visits exist, they're listed as sources ("found, but not a pre-op
    evaluation"), so the nurse sees why they didn't count.
  - No procedure date → the window check is marked not_evaluated, but presence
    is still checked. "There is no H&P at all" is true with or without a date.

  3. check_consent
  - The newest consent wins, by document date. A pending consent from 02/18
    followed by a signed one from 03/03 passes (cases 00007, 00049), and the
    passed check lists both so the replacement is visible.
  - It must be patient_signed and matches_scheduled_procedure. A signed consent
    for the wrong surgery fails (case 00024).
  - Two consents on the same date → the signed-and-matching one is preferred, so
    the result doesn't depend on the order the LLM listed them.
  - No consent → REQUIRED_DOCUMENTATION.
  - There's no time window for consents; the policy doesn't set one.

  4. check_labs
  - Required tests come from POLICY.required_labs[risk]. There's one finding per
    required test, so a HIGH-risk case gets separate CBC and CMP results.
  - Codes go through canonical_lab (LAB-CBC → CBC; BMP never counts as CMP), and
    entered-in-error results are skipped. Skipped results are listed in the
    evidence ("ignored CBC 02/10: entered-in-error"), so the nurse can see the
    fallback to the older result (cases 00004, 00041).
  - The most recent result per test passes if 0 ≤ days ≤ limit (≤30, or ≤14 for
    HIGH).
  - No usable result → REQUIRED_TESTING.
  - No risk level → not_evaluated; the required-fields rule already raised the
    issue.
  - No procedure date → presence is still checked, and the window is
    not_evaluated.

  Lab sources use the real lab id with a quote built from the raw fields, e.g.
  "LAB-CBC · Complete Blood Count w/ Differential (final)".

  Tests: tests/test_rules.py (no API calls)

  Small hand-built charts, each named after the case it mirrors where that
  helps:

  ┌───────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │   Area    │                        Cases tested                         │
  ├───────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Dates     │ Both date formats parse; a garbage date returns None; day   │
  │           │ counts across a month boundary                              │
  ├───────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Required  │ Missing date; missing risk; note date conflicts with the    │
  │ fields    │ structured date (both sources); note date agrees            │
  ├───────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │           │ 30 days passes, 31 fails; only a wellness visit → issue     │
  │ H&P       │ listing it; newest pre-op H&P chosen over an older one; no  │
  │           │ date → presence checked, window not_evaluated               │
  ├───────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │           │ Pending then signed → pass; signed then pending → fail;     │
  │ Consent   │ wrong procedure → fail; none → fail; same-date tie doesn't  │
  │           │ depend on order                                             │
  ├───────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │           │ LOW: 30 passes / 31 fails; HIGH: CBC at 14 passes / 15      │
  │ Labs      │ fails; CMP missing → fail; BMP doesn't count as CMP;        │
  │           │ entered-in-error skipped with fallback to the older result, │
  │           │  shown in evidence; LAB-CBC alias; no risk → not_evaluated  │
  ├───────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Registry  │ evaluate_rules runs all four rules and splits the results   │
  │           │ into issues and checks                                      │
  └───────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Not in this chunk: anticoagulation, BP and temperature (including
  missing-vitals detection), structured medications added to
  ignored_medications, the decision, and the coverage test. Those are chunk 5.

  The commit message will be feat: add rule engine with documentation and
  testing rules. Approve, or tell me what to change. The consent tie-break and
  "newest consent wins" are the two judgment calls most worth a second look.

⏺ Chunk 4 is written and tested: 123 tests pass (39 new). Nothing is committed
  yet.

  The headline result: on all 50 sample cases, the lab rule agrees with the
  human labels 50/50 on REQUIRED_TESTING, with no LLM involved; the baseline got
  this category wrong in several cases. The missing-field checks flagged
  exactly four cases (00011 and 00044 have no procedure date, 00029 and 00039
  have no risk), and the human labels mark all four as missing data.

  What the code does

  rules.py applies the policy in plain Python. Rules look only at the submission
  and at facts that already passed quote verification; they never call the LLM.

  - Chart holds what a rule is allowed to see: the submission and the verified
    facts. It also provides shortcuts for the procedure, its parsed date, and
    documents by id.
  - RULES and evaluate_rules are the registry. Every rule has the same shape,
    (chart, policy) → findings, and the engine runs them all and sorts the
    results into issues and checks. Adding a rule means writing one function and
    adding it to the list.
  - Helpers: parse_date (an unreadable date becomes "missing" instead of a
    crash), days_before, and small builders for issues, checks and sources, so
    every finding has the same shape.

  The four rules:

  Rule: check_required_fields
  Passes when: The procedure date and risk are present, and no note gives a
  different date
  Otherwise: MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA: missing for a blank field, conflict (with
  both sources) when a note disagrees
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Rule: check_hp
  Passes when: The newest pre-op H&P is 0–30 days before surgery
  Otherwise: REQUIRED_DOCUMENTATION. Wellness visits are listed as "found but
  not
  pre-op". With no procedure date, presence is still checked and the window is
  marked not_evaluated.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Rule: check_consent
  Passes when: The newest consent is signed by the patient and for this
  procedure
  Otherwise: REQUIRED_DOCUMENTATION, saying exactly what's wrong ("not signed by

  the patient", "names coronary artery bypass, not …")
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Rule: check_labs
  Passes when: The newest usable result for each required test is within its
  window
  Otherwise: REQUIRED_TESTING, one finding per test. Skipped results
  (entered-in-error or undated) are listed as evidence.

  Each finding carries computed values such as days_before: 31, limit_days: 30,
  so the nurse sees the arithmetic, not just the verdict.

  Two differences from the approved plan

  1. A result dated after the surgery gets readable wording ("3 days after the
     procedure") from a small shared helper, instead of "-3 days before". It
     still fails, since it can't count as pre-op.
  2. Undated lab results are skipped and listed as evidence, the same way as
     entered-in-error results. The plan didn't cover them; without this they'd
     have crashed the rule.

  Your two judgment calls, as built

  - The newest consent wins, even when it's worse than an older one. If a signed
    consent is followed by a newer pending one, the case fails. A newer pending
    consent suggests re-consent is underway, so the older one shouldn't be
    trusted. There's a test for each direction.
  - Same-day tie: the valid consent is preferred, so the result doesn't depend
    on the order the LLM listed them in. The test runs both orders.

  How I checked it

  - tests/test_rules.py (39 tests) covers every row in the approved test table:
    the 30/31 and 14/15 day boundaries, a gap that crosses February, BMP not
    counting as CMP, the entered-in-error fallback, superseded consents, the
    date conflict, and the not-evaluated paths.
  - Mutation check: I deliberately broke seven pieces of logic. Examples: making
    the window exclusive, letting wellness visits count, ignoring the procedure
    match, counting entered-in-error results, using the oldest lab. Each break
    was caught by the test written for it.
  - pyflakes found no unused code. The new files are formatted with ruff; one
    93-character line remains because it's a single string.

  The commit message will be feat: add rule engine with documentation and
  testing rules. Reply "approve" and I'll commit and push.

  vitals), structured medications added to ignored_medications, the final
  decision, and the "every rule always appears" test.

⏺ Chunk 4 is committed and pushed (08ae63e). Four of nine chunks are done.

  Here's the plan for chunk 5: the second half of the rules. Nothing changes
  until you approve. This chunk has more judgment calls than the others, marked
  ⚖️. Those are the parts worth reading closely.

  Chunk 5: anticoagulation, vitals, decision, coverage

  1. check_anticoagulation (Rule 3)

  Step 1: work out each anticoagulant's status. Drugs are grouped by generic
  name, so "Xarelto" and "rivaroxaban" count as the same drug. The status
  combines two kinds of source:
  - the structured med list: active true means taking, false means stopped,
    blank means unknown
  - the verified note mentions: the most recent note by document date counts

  ⚖️ How the two sources combine:

  ┌──────────────┬───────────────┬─────────────────────┬────────────────────┐
  │ Structured   │  Latest note  │       Result        │      Example       │
  │     list     │               │                     │                    │
  ├──────────────┼───────────────┼─────────────────────┼────────────────────┤
  │              │ says          │ uncertain (the note │                    │
  │ any          │ uncertain     │  explicitly doubts  │ 00033              │
  │              │               │ it)                 │                    │
  ├──────────────┼───────────────┼─────────────────────┼────────────────────┤
  │ taking       │ stopped (or   │ conflict            │ —                  │
  │              │ the reverse)  │                     │                    │
  ├──────────────┼───────────────┼─────────────────────┼────────────────────┤
  │              │ taking or     │                     │ 00000 (only in a   │
  │ any          │ stopped       │ the note's status   │ note), 00018       │
  │              │               │                     │ (switch)           │
  ├──────────────┼───────────────┼─────────────────────┼────────────────────┤
  │ taking or    │ no mention    │ the list's status   │ most cases; 00043  │
  │ stopped      │               │                     │ (inactive)         │
  ├──────────────┼───────────────┼─────────────────────┼────────────────────┤
  │ blank        │ no mention    │ uncertain           │ —                  │
  └──────────────┴───────────────┴─────────────────────┴────────────────────┘

  Step 2: one finding per drug.
  - Taking → needs a plan for that same drug with both a before-surgery and an
    after-surgery instruction. A warfarin plan doesn't cover apixaban
    (case 00018).
    - A complete plan → passed check, citing the plan's quotes.
    - Otherwise → ANTICOAGULATION_MANAGEMENT, saying what's missing ("no plan",
      or "plan has no after-surgery instruction", as in 00020).
  - Stopped → not_applicable check, e.g. "Warfarin is not currently taken", with
    sources.
  - Uncertain → MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA (reason missing).
  - Conflict → MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA (reason conflict) with both sources.
  - No anticoagulant anywhere → one not_applicable check.

  ⚖️ When there are several plans for the same drug: any verified complete plan
  passes, and every plan is listed as evidence. The alternative is "the newest
  plan decides", but newer notes often just restate a plan partially ("per
  cardiology plan"), which would cause false NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP results. The cost:
  a later "hold and reassess" note wouldn't override an older complete plan. I
  think that's rare, and the nurse sees all plans either way.

  Also: non-anticoagulant drugs from the structured med list are added to
  ignored_medications (source medications[3], quote_verified: true since it's
  structured data). This is a separate function,
  ignored_structured_medications(), not a rule, because it doesn't produce
  findings.

  2. check_blood_pressure and check_temperature (Rule 4)

  Readings come from both the structured vitals and the verified note vitals.
  - Note readings: the date is measured_on, or the document's date if the note
    doesn't state one, plus measured_time if stated.
  - Structured readings: use their full timestamp.
  - Celsius is converted to Fahrenheit in code and rounded to 0.1, so 37.9 °C
    becomes 100.2 °F.

  ⚖️ Choosing "the most recent" reading:
  - On the latest date, if every reading has a time and they're all from the
    same kind of source, the latest time wins. Case 00016: the 08:30 recheck
    (137/78) beats the 08:06 reading (189/112).
  - Otherwise the most severe reading on that date wins: the one closest to or
    over a limit. This is your earlier choice for when readings can't be
    ordered.
  - Why "same kind of source": structured times are UTC (…Z), while note times
    are local clock times with no time zone. Comparing 10:12 UTC with 08:30
    local could put them in the wrong order, so mixed same-day readings use the
    cautious rule.

  Results:
  - Systolic ≥ 180 or diastolic ≥ 110, or temperature > 100.4 °F →
    ACUTE_SAFETY_EXCLUSION, citing the deciding reading.
  - Otherwise → passed check. Older readings it replaced are listed as evidence,
    so "189/112 replaced by a 137/78 recheck" is visible.
  - No BP, or no temperature, anywhere (structured or notes) →
    MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA (cases 00009, 00013, 00021, 00022).
  - Two readings at exactly the same date and time with different values →
    MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA (reason conflict) with both sources. Duplicates with
    the same values aren't a conflict; notes often restate the structured
    vitals.
  - A BP reading missing one of its numbers is skipped and listed as evidence.
  - ⚖️ 38.0 °C converts to exactly 100.4 °F, which is not a fever. The policy
    says "> 100.4".

  3. decide(issues)

  - Any ACUTE_SAFETY_EXCLUSION → NOT_CLEARED
  - Otherwise, any issue → NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP
  - Otherwise → READY

  It reads only the issue categories, so the LLM can't affect it. The fail-safe
  floor ("never better than NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP when something broke") goes in the
  wiring chunk, where failures are caught.

  4. Registry

  RULES becomes: required_fields, hp, consent, labs, anticoagulation,
  blood_pressure, temperature.

  Tests (added to tests/test_rules.py, no API calls)

  Area: Anticoagulant status
  Cases tested: Note-only Xarelto counts as taking; inactive warfarin plus a
  "stopped" note means stopped; unknown plus an "uncertain" note is missing
  data; list says taking but note says stopped is a conflict; an older "taking"
   note followed by a newer "switched" note means stopped; brand and generic
  names grouped together
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Area: Plans
  Cases tested: Complete plan passes; missing after-surgery instruction fails; a

  warfarin plan doesn't cover apixaban; aspirin-only patient is not applicable;
   two plans where only one is complete still passes
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Area: Ignored meds
  Cases tested: Structured aspirin and tamsulosin recorded with medications[i]
  sources
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Area: BP
  Cases tested: 179/109 passes; 180 and 110 each fail; a later timed recheck
  replaces a high reading; untimed same-day readings → most severe wins; mixed
  UTC and note times → most severe wins; a later recheck still over the limit
  fails (00032, 00046); no BP is missing data; same timestamp with different
  values is a conflict
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Area: Temperature
  Cases tested: 100.4 °F passes, 100.5 °F fails; 38.6 °C fails; 37.9 °C passes;
  38.0 °C passes; a note newer than the structured vitals wins (00035); no
  temperature is missing data
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Area: Decision
  Cases tested: Exclusion plus other issues → NOT_CLEARED; issues only →
  NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP; none → READY
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Area: Coverage
  Cases tested: For several very different charts (including a completely empty
  one), every rule except system appears at least once in the issues or checks

  Real-data check: I'll run the anticoagulation and vitals rules on all 50 cases
  using structured data only (no notes yet). It won't match the labels
  everywhere, since many deciding facts live in notes, but it will show which
  cases depend on extraction. That makes a useful baseline for chunk 6.

  the "any complete plan passes" choice matter most for accuracy.

          +e.quote))

          +c >= policy.diastolic_hold_at

          +nding]:                                                       
      616 +    """Rule 4: the most recent temperature must not exceed the
          + fever threshold."""                                          
      617 +                                                              
      618 +    readings: list[_Reading] = []                             
      619 +    skipped: list[Source] = []                                
      620 +    for index, vital in enumerate(chart.submission.vitals):   
      621 +        if not isinstance(vital, TemperatureVital):           
      622 +            continue                                          
      623 +        source = Source(                                      
      624 +            kind="vital",                                     
      625 +            id=f"vitals[{index}]",                            
      626 +            date=vital.date,                                  
      627 +            quote=f"Temp {vital.value_f} F ({vital.source})", 
      628 +        )                                                     
      629 +        day, at = _parse_timestamp(vital.date)                
      630 +        if day is None or vital.value_f is None:              
      631 +            skipped.append(source)                            
      632 +        else:                                                 
      633 +            readings.append(_Reading(day, at, "utc", (float(vi
          +tal.value_f),), source))                                      
      634 +    for note in chart.facts.note_vitals:                      
      635 +        if note.kind != "temperature":                        
      636 +            continue                                          
      637 +        temperature_f = _to_fahrenheit(note.temperature, note.
          +temperature_unit)                                             
      638 +        reading = _note_reading(chart, note, (temperature_f,))
      639 +        if reading is None:                                   
      640 +            skipped.append(_doc_source(chart, note.doc_id, not
          +e.quote))                                                     
      641 +        else:                                                 
      642 +            readings.append(reading)                          
      643 +                                                              
      644 +    return _check_vital(                                      
      645 +        chart,                                                
      646 +        "temperature",                                        
      647 +        "temperature",                                        
      648 +        readings,                                             
      649 +        skipped,                                              
      650 +        lambda r: r.values[0] > policy.temp_f_hold_above,     
      651 +        lambda r: r.values[0],                                
      652 +        lambda r: f"{r.values[0]:g} F",                       
      653 +        f"hold above {policy.temp_f_hold_above:g} F",         
      654 +    )                                                         
      655 +                                                              
      656 +                                                              
      657 +def _to_fahrenheit(value: float | None, unit: str | None) -> f
          +loat | None:                                                  
      658 +    if value is None or unit is None:                         
      659 +        return None                                           
      660 +    # Rounded so 38.0 C is exactly 100.4 F rather than a float
          + just above or below it.                                      
      661 +    return round(value * 9 / 5 + 32, 1) if unit == "C" else va
          +lue                                                           
      662 +                                                              
      663 +                                                              
      664 +def _parse_timestamp(value: str | None) -> tuple[date | None, 
          +time | None]:                                                 
      665 +    """Split "2026-02-24T10:12:00Z" into its date and time; a 
          +bare date has no time."""                                     
      666 +                                                              
      667 +    day = parse_date(value)                                   
      668 +    if day is None or value is None or "T" not in value:      
      669 +        return day, None                                      
      670 +    try:                                                      
      671 +        return day, datetime.fromisoformat(value.strip()).time
          +()                                                            
      672 +    except ValueError:                                        
      673 +        return day, None                                      
      674 +                                                              
      675 +                                                              
      676 +def _note_reading(                                            
      677 +    chart: Chart, note: NoteVital, values: tuple[float | None,
          + ...]                                                         
      678 +) -> _Reading | None:                                         
      679 +    """A note vital dated by its own text if stated, else by i
          +ts document."""                                               
      680 +                                                              
      681 +    day = parse_date(note.measured_on) or _doc_date(chart, not
          +e.doc_id)                                                     
      682 +    if day is None or any(value is None for value in values): 
      683 +        return None                                           
      684 +    try:                                                      
      685 +        at = time.fromisoformat(note.measured_time) if note.me
          +asured_time else None                                         
      686 +    except ValueError:                                        
      687 +        at = None                                             
      688 +    complete = tuple(float(value) for value in values if value
          + is not None)                                                 
      689 +    return _Reading(day, at, "note", complete, _doc_source(cha
          +rt, note.doc_id, note.quote))                                 
      690 +                                                              
      691 +                                                              
      692 +def _check_vital(                                             
      693 +    chart: Chart,                                             
      694 +    rule: RuleId,                                             
      695 +    label: str,                                               
      696 +    readings: list[_Reading],                                 
      697 +    skipped: list[Source],                                    
      698 +    exceeds: Callable[[_Reading], bool],                      
      699 +    severity: Callable[[_Reading], float],                    
      700 +    describe: Callable[[_Reading], str],                      
      701 +    thresholds: str,                                          
      702 +) -> list[Finding]:                                           
      703 +    """Pick the most recent reading (most severe when same-day
          + order is unknowable)."""                                     
      704 +                                                              
      705 +    if not readings:                                          
      706 +        finding = f"No usable {label} reading in the structure
          +d vitals or notes."                                           
      707 +        return [_issue("MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA", rule, finding,
          + skipped, "missing")]                                         
      708 +                                                              
      709 +    latest_day = max(r.day for r in readings)                 
      710 +    same_day = [r for r in readings if r.day == latest_day]   
      711 +    orderable = all(r.at is not None for r in same_day) and le
          +n({r.clock for r in same_day}) == 1                           
      712 +    if orderable:                                             
      713 +        latest_at = max(r.at for r in same_day if r.at is not 
          +None)                                                         
      714 +        candidates = [r for r in same_day if r.at == latest_at
          +]                                                             
      715 +    else:                                                     
      716 +        candidates = same_day                                 
      717 +    chosen = max(candidates, key=severity)                    
      718 +    others = sorted(                                          
      719 +        (r for r in readings if r is not chosen),             
      720 +        key=lambda r: (r.day, r.at or time.min),              
      721 +        reverse=True,                                         
      722 +    )                                                         
      723 +    sources = [chosen.source, *(r.source for r in others), *sk
          +ipped]                                                        
      724 +                                                              
      725 +    findings: list[Finding] = []                              
      726 +    by_moment: defaultdict[tuple[time, str], set[tuple[float, 
          +...]]] = defaultdict(set)                                     
      727 +    for r in same_day:                                        
      728 +        if r.at is not None:                                  
      729 +            by_moment[(r.at, r.clock)].add(r.values)          
      730 +    if any(len(values) > 1 for values in by_moment.values()): 
      731 +        finding = f"Different {label} values are recorded for 
          +the same time on {latest_day}."                               
      732 +        findings.append(                                      
      733 +            _issue("MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA", rule, finding, [r.
          +source for r in same_day], "conflict")                        
      734 +        )                                                     
      735 +                                                              
      736 +    when = f"{chosen.day}" + (f" {chosen.at:%H:%M}" if chosen.
          +at else "")                                                   
      737 +    how = (                                                   
      738 +        ""                                                    
      739 +        if orderable or len(same_day) == 1                    
      740 +        else " Same-day readings could not be put in order, so
          + the most severe was used."                                   
      741 +    )                                                         
      742 +    computed: dict[str, ComputedValue] = {                    
      743 +        "reading": describe(chosen),                          
      744 +        "measured_on": chosen.day.isoformat(),                
      745 +        "thresholds": thresholds,                             
      746 +    }                                                         
      747 +    if exceeds(chosen):                                       
      748 +        finding = f"Most recent {label} {describe(chosen)} ({w
          +hen}) meets the {thresholds}.{how}"                           
      749 +        findings.append(_issue("ACUTE_SAFETY_EXCLUSION", rule,
          + finding, sources, **computed))                               
      750 +    else:                                                     
      751 +        finding = f"Most recent {label} {describe(chosen)} ({w
          +hen}) is within limits ({thresholds}).{how}"                  
      752 +        findings.append(_check(rule, "passed", finding, source
          +s, **computed))                                               
      753 +    return findings                                           
      754 +                                                              
      755 +                                                              
      756 +def decide(issues: Sequence[TriageIssue]) -> Decision:        
      757 +    """Derive the decision from issue categories alone."""    
      758 +                                                              
      759 +    if any(issue.category == "ACUTE_SAFETY_EXCLUSION" for issu
          +e in issues):                                                 
      760 +        return "NOT_CLEARED"                                  
      761 +    return "NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP" if issues else "READY"           
      762 +                                                              
      763 +                                                              
      764  # -------------------------
      765  # Engine
      766  # -------------------------
     ...
      770      check_hp,
      771      check_consent,
      772      check_labs,
      773 +    check_anticoagulation,                                    
      774 +    check_blood_pressure,                                     
      775 +    check_temperature,                                        
      776  )
      777  
      778

      627 +def _bp_vital(                                                
      628 +    systolic: float, diastolic: float, days: int, at: str = "1
          +0:00"                                                         
      629 +) -> dict[str, Any]:

⏺ Chunk 5 is written, reviewed and tested. Nothing is committed yet. 165 tests
  pass (42 new).

  What was added to rules.py

  check_anticoagulation (Rule 3)
  - Groups medications by generic drug, so "Coumadin" and "warfarin" count as
    one drug.
  - Works out each drug's status from the medication list plus the most recent
    note that mentions it, following the approved table:
    - an explicit "uncertain" in that note → uncertain
    - the list and the note disagree → conflict
    - otherwise the note's status if there is one, else the list's
  - Gives one finding per drug:
    - stopped → not_applicable
    - uncertain → MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA
    - conflict → MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA, with both sources
    - taking → needs one plan for that drug covering both before and after
      surgery
  - When a plan fails, the finding says exactly what's missing: "no
    perioperative plan", "no after-surgery instruction", or "no single plan
    covers both".
  - ignored_structured_medications() lists every non-anticoagulant drug on the
    medication list, completing your "nothing dropped silently" requirement.

  check_blood_pressure and check_temperature (Rule 4)
  - Combine structured vitals and verified note vitals. Note readings are dated
    by their own text if stated, otherwise by their document. Celsius is
    converted in code.
  - The most recent reading decides:
    - the latest time wins when every reading that day is timed and on the same
      clock
    - otherwise the most severe reading that day wins, and the finding says so
  - Readings that were replaced are still listed as evidence: "137/78 at 08:30",
    followed by the 189/112 it replaced.
  - No reading at all → MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA. Different values at the same
    moment → a conflict issue.

  decide(issues): safety exclusion → NOT_CLEARED; any other issue →
  NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP; otherwise READY.

  Differences from the plan

  1. A temperature without a unit is skipped, not guessed. I could have treated
     37.1 as obviously Celsius, but guessing goes against the "LLM reports, code
     decides" principle. If it's the only reading, the result is
     MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA, so a nurse looks at it.
  2. Same-time vital conflicts are only checked on the latest day. Conflicting
     old readings can't affect the decision, so flagging them would only add
     noise.

  Review fixes during this chunk

  - Removed an unused chart parameter from _check_vital. pyflakes doesn't catch
    unused parameters; I found it by re-reading.
  - Collapsed a duplicated missing-value check in _note_reading.
  - Rewrote the status-combining logic so each line maps to one row of the
    approved table, and split two long lines.

  How I tested it

  - 42 new tests, covering every row of the approved test table. They include
    the coverage invariant: for four very different charts (empty, no date or
    risk, fully ready, high-risk + anticoagulated + hypertensive), every rule
    appears in the output.
  - Mutation check: I deliberately broke 13 pieces of logic, for example making
    a threshold inclusive or exclusive, removing the Celsius rounding, ignoring
    the clock difference, letting another drug's plan count, accepting half a
    plan, or dropping a rule from the registry. 12 were caught. The 13th can't
    change any result: after the conflict check, at most one source has a known
    status, so swapping the two lines has no effect.
    - The Celsius rounding was confirmed necessary: two tests fail without it.
  - Structured-data-only comparison on all 50 cases: wherever the deciding
    information is in structured fields, the rules agree with the human labels.

⏺ Useful check: the Responses API has no seed parameter (OpenAI SDK 3.41). It
  does support temperature, timeout, store and client-level retries. Here's the
  plan adjusted to match.

  Chunk 6: extraction.py, the only code that calls the LLM

  What it does

  def extract_facts(submission, *, model, client=None, cache_dir=DEFAULT_CACHE)
  -> ExtractedFacts

  It sends the documents to OpenAI and returns ExtractedFacts. Any failure (API
  error, timeout, refusal, malformed output) raises one exception type,
  ExtractionError, with a clear message. The wiring chunk turns that into a
  fail-safe NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP. This module never decides anything: it returns raw
  facts, and verify.py and rules.py take it from there.

  What the model sees, and what it doesn't

  Sent: each document's doc_id, type, date and text, plus the scheduled
  procedure_type. The procedure type is needed to judge whether a consent
  matches.

  Deliberately not sent:

  ┌────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │      Not sent      │                        Why                         │
  ├────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Patient name, MRN, │ Extraction doesn't need them. Sending less         │
  │  DOB, sex          │ identifying health data is good practice and a     │
  │                    │ strong write-up point.                             │
  ├────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                    │ We use note dates to check this field for          │
  │ procedure_date     │ conflicts. If the model saw it, it might echo it   │
  │                    │ back and hide a real conflict.                     │
  ├────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                    │ The model's job is reading free text; code reads   │
  │ Structured meds,   │ the structured data. Showing the med list would    │
  │ vitals, labs       │ tempt it to copy statuses instead of reading the   │
  │                    │ notes.                                             │
  └────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  The last two differ from the original plan, which included the med list. I
  think leaving them out is better: the model can't anchor on values it's being
  checked against.

  The prompt

  A short system prompt. Most per-field guidance already lives in the schema
  descriptions from chunk 2, so it isn't duplicated here. The prompt covers:
  - Role: "You extract facts from clinical documents. You do not decide whether
    the patient is ready for surgery."
  - Quotes: copied exactly, one sentence or less, containing the extracted
    value, never paraphrased, never stitched together from two places.
  - Completeness: one item per fact. Every BP and temperature reading becomes
    its own item, including rechecks (with their times). Every medication
    mention, including drugs missing from any list.
  - No inference: if a document doesn't state something, leave it out. Don't
    fill in values from medical knowledge. Report the unit as written.

  The API call

  Setting: Method
  Value: client.responses.parse(text_format=ExtractedFacts)
  Why: The SDK sends our schema in strict mode and parses the response into
  Pydantic models
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Setting: temperature
  Value: 0
  Why: As deterministic as the API allows
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Setting: Seed
  Value: not available
  Why: The Responses API has no seed. The SDK check above confirmed it.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Setting: store
  Value: False
  Why: Don't keep patient data on OpenAI's side after the call
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Setting: Client
  Value: one shared OpenAI(max_retries=3, timeout=60)
  Why: Reused across calls instead of a new client per call (one of the
  baseline's issues). The SDK retries rate limits, 5xx errors and connection
  failures with backoff.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Setting: Refusal / empty output
  Value: ExtractionError
  Why: Never treated as "no facts found", which could otherwise look like a
  clean
  chart

  Determinism without a seed comes from two things: temperature=0 for the call,
  and the disk cache for repeat runs.

  Disk cache

  - Location: .cache/extractions/<sha256>.json, added to .gitignore.
  - Key: a hash of the model, the prompt, the schema and the exact input.
    Changing any of them automatically invalidates old entries.
  - Behavior: a cache hit means no API call, so a re-run gives identical facts
    and costs nothing. This is what makes the chunk 8 iteration loop cheap.
  - An unreadable or corrupt cache file is ignored and re-fetched. It can never
    crash a run.
  - cache_dir=None turns caching off. The determinism check in chunk 9 uses this
    so it measures the model, not the cache.

  Tests: tests/test_extraction.py (mocked client, no API calls)

  - Request shape: right model, prompt, temperature=0, store=False,
    text_format=ExtractedFacts
  - Payload: includes doc ids, texts and the procedure type; excludes patient
    name, MRN, DOB and procedure date
  - Cache: the second call doesn't hit the API; a different model or input means
    a different cache entry; a corrupt cache file is re-fetched; cache_dir=None
    always calls the API
  - Failures: an API exception, a refusal, or empty output each raise
    ExtractionError, and nothing is written to the cache

  Live check (about 5 real API calls)

  I'll run extraction, verification and the rules on five cases from chunk 5's
  list of note-dependent mismatches, picked to cover each extraction challenge:

  ┌───────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐

  │ 00016 │ High BP followed by a timed recheck in an addendum               │
  ├───────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 00027 │ Eliquis mentioned only in a nursing note, no plan                │
  ├───────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 00037 │ Xarelto plan by brand name                                       │
  ├───────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 00004 │ Warfarin plan with an enoxaparin bridge that must not become a   │

  For each one, I'll show the facts extracted, how many were discarded by quote
  verification, and whether the final decision and categories match the label.
  The discard rate tells us whether the quote prompt works before we spend 50
  calls.

  on is leaving the procedure date and medication list out of the prompt.

⏺ Writing extraction.py. One design detail differs from what I'd first sketched:
  documents are sent as plain text blocks, not JSON. In JSON, line breaks
  become literal \n sequences, the model might copy those into its quotes, and
  quote verification would then fail. Plain text means the model sees exactly
  the characters it has to quote.

  What's ready to review in the meantime

  extraction.py is the only module that talks to the LLM.

  - build_extraction_input sends the scheduled procedure plus each document as a
    plain-text block. As discussed, it leaves out patient identifiers, the
    procedure date and the structured medication list. One change from the plan:
    documents are sent as plain text instead of JSON. In JSON, line breaks
    become \n escape sequences, and if the model copied those into a quote, the
    quote check would wrongly fail.
  - extract_facts checks the disk cache first. On a miss it calls the API, then
    caches the result.
  - _call_model calls responses.parse with the strict schema, temperature=0 and
    store=False. Any failure, or an empty or refused response, becomes
    ExtractionError, so a failure can never look like "a clean chart with no
    facts". This is the one place I catch every exception type, on purpose: each
    kind of failure has to reach the fail-safe.
  - The client is created once and reused, with 3 retries and a 60-second
    timeout.
  - The cache is keyed by a hash of the model, prompt, schema and input. Writes
    are atomic (a temp file, then rename), so a crash can't leave a half-written
    entry. A corrupt entry is simply fetched again.
  - .gitignore now ignores .cache/.

  tests/test_extraction.py, 14 tests with a fake client:
  - the request uses strict mode, temperature=0 and store=False
  - document text reaches the model unchanged
  - name, MRN, date of birth, procedure date and medication list are not sent (6
    checks)
  - the cache works: hits, model- and input-specific keys, corrupt-entry
    recovery, cache off
  - an API error or a refusal raises ExtractionError and caches nothing

  Here's the plan for chunk 7: connecting everything, plus the fail-safe. This
  is where your "never fail open" requirement gets implemented. Nothing changes
  until you approve.

  Chunk 7: pipeline.py

  The flow

  triage_submission(submission, model=...)            ← unchanged signature,
  still in core.py
    └─ pipeline.triage(...)
         1. validate input        → PatientSubmission
         2. extract facts (LLM)   → ExtractedFacts           (extraction.py)
         3. verify quotes         → verified facts + issues  (verify.py)
         4. apply rules           → issues + checks          (rules.py)
         5. decide + explain      → TriageOutput

  triage_submission keeps its exact signature, so run_baseline.py and
  run_evals.py need no changes. It hands off to pipeline.py through a deferred
  import. That's needed because rules.py already imports from core.py; a normal
  import back from core.py would create a circular import. A comment will
  explain this.

  Fail-safe: what happens at each failure point

  Failure: Input doesn't match the expected shape
  What runs: Nothing; there's no valid data to check
  Result: NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP, one system issue (invalid_submission) with the
  validation error
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Failure: Extraction fails (API error, timeout, refusal)
  What runs: Only rules that need no document text: labs and required fields. A
  structured vital over the limit still counts.
  Result: At least NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP, with a system issue (extraction_failed) that

  includes the error. The H&P, consent, anticoagulation, BP and temperature
  rules each appear as not_evaluated: document extraction failed. Can still be
  NOT_CLEARED if structured vitals show a hold.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Failure: A quote doesn't verify
  What runs: Everything; that fact is discarded
  Result: At least NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP (from chunk 3)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Failure: Unexpected bug in our own code
  What runs: Caught at the top
  Result: NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP, a system issue (internal_error) with the exception
  type and message

  Why BP and temperature are not_evaluated when extraction fails, even if the
  structured readings are normal: a note might contain a newer, abnormal reading
  (case 00002). A "passed" check would claim more than we know. The exception
  is a structured reading that's already over a limit; that is real evidence of
  danger, so it's kept.

  None of these paths can produce READY or a crash, and the output always
  validates against TriageOutput.

  Assembling the output

  - issues: verification issues plus rule issues (plus any system issue)
  - checks: from the rules
  - ignored_medications: from verification (note mentions) plus structured
    non-anticoagulants
  - decision: from decide(issues), the category-only function from chunk 5
  - explanation: built from a template, not written by the LLM. For example:
    "NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP: 2 blocking issues. REQUIRED_DOCUMENTATION: Most recent
    consent (2026-02-24) is not signed by the patient. … 5 checks passed." It
    only restates the issues and checks, so it can't contradict them.

  Removing dead code from core.py

  Once the pipeline replaces the baseline, these are no longer used anywhere:
  BASELINE_SYSTEM_PROMPT, build_user_prompt, triage_output_json_schema, and the
  old single-call body of triage_submission. I'll delete them. The policy text
  now lives in policy.py as data, and the old prompt would be a second,
  out-of-date copy of it. The git history keeps the original.

  Tests

  tests/test_triage_submission.py is rewritten. The 3 old tests check the
  removed baseline call (strict=False, the policy prompt), so they no longer
  apply. New tests, with a fake extraction client:

  - End to end:
    - a clean chart → READY, all 7 rules present as checks, the explanation
      lists them
    - a chart with problems → the decision and categories come only from the
      rules
    - ignored medications from both sources appear in the output
  - Fail-safe (one test per row of the table):
    - invalid input → NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP, no crash
    - an API error → NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP; note-dependent rules marked not_evaluated;
      never READY
    - an API error plus a structured BP of 200/120 → still NOT_CLEARED
    - a refusal → NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP
    - a bad quote → NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP
    - a crashing rule (simulated) → internal_error, NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP, no crash
  - Invariant: every output on every path validates, and no failure path returns
    READY.

  Split into two commits

  I'll keep the report-viewer update separate. It's UI code, independent of the
  pipeline logic:
  - 7a: pipeline and fail-safe (above). Message: feat: wire extraction,
    verification, and rules into a fail-safe pipeline
  - 7b: view_report.py shows each issue's reason, finding and sources, plus the
    passed checks and ignored medications. I'll describe that one separately.

  Approve 7a and I'll build it, test it, and show you the results before
  committing.

  Why the remaining 13 cases fail

  All 13 trace back to the LLM extraction step. None are mistakes in the rules.

  Cause: The model garbles the degree sign
  Cases: 00003, 00010, 00017, 00019, 00035, 00047, 00049
  What happens: The document says 98.1 °F, but the model's quote comes back as
  98.1 \x176F (a control character plus a stray "6"). The quote no longer
  matches the document, so the check discards the fact.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Cause: The model joins pieces of a sentence
  Cases: 00006, 00034, 00038
  What happens: The document says Vitals 10:05 BP 129/71 mmHg, HR 70, T 98.5 F,
  and the model quotes Vitals 10:05 T 98.5 F, skipping the middle. That isn't
  an exact quote, so it's discarded.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Cause: Notes that only mention an H&P were counted as H&Ps
  Cases: 00013, 00048
  What happens: An anesthesia note saying "H&P: not yet on file" was marked as a

  pre-op H&P
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Cause: A "we'll decide later" note was counted as an instruction
  Cases: 00020
  What happens: "Post-operative anticoagulation will be addressed after surgery"

  was taken as a resume instruction

  The quote check did exactly what you specified: it threw away every fact it
  couldn't confirm. The cost is that 10 of the 13 cases lost real vital-sign
  readings. In 00047 a genuine BP of 161/117 was dropped, so we returned
  NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP where the correct answer was NOT_CLEARED. That's the cautious
  direction, but not the right answer.

  What's ready to commit

  7a: pipeline and fail-safe, in pipeline.py, core.py and
  tests/test_triage_submission.py
  - Each case goes through four steps: extract facts, check the quotes, apply
    the rules, decide.
  - Invalid input → NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP, and the model is never called.
  - The LLM call fails or refuses → NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP. The rules that depend on
    documents are marked not_evaluated, but a structured BP of 200/120 still
    produces NOT_CLEARED.
  - An unexpected bug → NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP with an internal_error reason, instead
    of crashing.
  - The explanation text is built from the issues and checks, so it can't
    contradict them.
  - I removed the old prompt-based baseline code from core.py (about 110 lines)
    because nothing uses it anymore.
  - Testing: I broke the safety logic on purpose 7 different ways, and the tests
    caught all 7.

  7b: report viewer, in view_report.py
  - Shows each issue's category, reason, finding and sources; every check with
    its evidence; and the ignored medications.
  - I checked it by rendering a real case from the report.

  Proposed chunk 8: fix the extraction problems

  All changes are to the extraction instructions or text cleanup. The rules
  don't change.

  1. Degree sign. Remove ° from the text sent to the model, and ignore ° in the
     quote check (98.1 °F and 98.1 F mean the same thing). This fixes the most
     common failure.
  2. Joined quotes. Tell the model to quote the whole vitals line and never skip
     words.
  3. H&P mistakes. Say explicitly that a note which mentions, requests, or
     reports a missing H&P is not an H&P, and that the quote should be the text
     that shows the document is an H&P.
  4. "Decide later" notes. Make the instruction about such notes stricter.

  These change the instructions, so all 50 cases will be re-extracted (about 50
  API calls). I'll then re-score, show the change from 87.33%, and check that no
  case that passed before now fails.

  One option I'm not proposing without your sign-off: letting quotes skip words
  using "…", with each piece still matched exactly. That would loosen your
  "quote must appear in the document" rule, so it's your call. I'd rather try
  fixing the instructions first.

  Approve the two commits, and chunk 8 as described.

❯ we need to address some specific bugs in the testing, what ideas do you have
  for going about that?

⏺ Here are my ideas for the four bugs the eval exposed, plus a process for
  fixing them without overfitting to 50 cases. If you meant different bugs, tell
  me which ones.

  Bug 1: the degree sign gets garbled (7 cases, the biggest win)

  Root cause: when the model writes ° inside its JSON output, it sometimes
  produces a broken character sequence (\x17 + 6). It's a model output glitch,
  and it happened in 7 of 50 cases.

  Option: A. Remove ° before the model sees the text (recommended)
  How it works: Send 98.1 F instead of 98.1 °F, and ignore ° when checking
  quotes
  Trade-off: Deterministic and tiny, and the symbol carries no information the
  unit doesn't. Removes the trigger rather than working around it.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Option: B. Convert all text to plain ASCII before sending
  How it works: Same idea, applied to every special character
  Trade-off: Covers future characters too, but changes more text than we have
  evidence for, and adds a dependency
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Option: C. Make the quote check tolerate junk characters
  How it works: Accept the garbled quote
  Trade-off: Doesn't work: the stray "6" turns 98.1 into 98.16

  Bug 2: quotes pieced together from separate parts of a line (3 cases)

  Option: A. Tighter instructions (recommended first)
  How it works: "Quote the whole vitals line; never skip words"
  Trade-off: Cheap, but the model may still slip sometimes
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Option: B. Cite by line number instead of copying text (best long-term fix)
  How it works: Number each document's lines; the model returns doc_id plus a
  line number, and code copies the quote from the document
  Trade-off: Quotes become exact by construction, so this whole class of bug
  disappears, and so does bug 1. The quote check becomes "are the claimed
  values on that line?" A bigger change to the schema and verification.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Option: C. Allow "…" between exact pieces
  How it works: Each piece must still match exactly, in order
  Trade-off: Loosens your exact-quote rule, so I wouldn't do it without your
  explicit OK

  Option B is the strongest idea here. The model can no longer misquote, only
  point at the wrong line, and the value check catches that.

  Bug 3: notes that mention an H&P are counted as H&Ps (2 cases)

  - A. Clearer instructions (recommended): "A note that mentions, requests, or
    says an H&P is missing is not an H&P." Also require the quote to be the text
    that shows the document is an H&P, such as its heading or "pre-op
    evaluation". That also fixes the weak evidence we saw in the viewer.
  - B. A second, independent check (defense in depth): add a fact type for
    statements like "H&P not yet on file". If a note says the H&P is missing and
    the chart still claims to have one, the case is flagged as a conflict. This
    catches the error even when the model mislabels the document, but it's one
    more fact type to maintain.

  Bug 4: "will be addressed after surgery" counted as a resume instruction (1
  case)

  - A. Stricter instructions (recommended): "Only an instruction that says when
    or how to restart. If restarting is left for later, return null."
  - B. A keyword check in code: require the after-surgery quote to contain a
    restart word ("resume", "restart") or a timing ("24-48 hours", "post-op day
    1"). It's deterministic, but word lists are brittle and could reject valid
    wording, so I'd only add it if A doesn't hold.

  Process: how to fix these without overfitting

  1. Write a test that reproduces each bug before fixing it, where code can be
     tested. For example: a document containing 98.1 °F and a quote 98.1 F
     should verify. Fails now, passes after the fix.
  2. Fix the root cause, never a single case. With only 50 cases, special-casing
     would inflate the score without helping real charts. Each fix should be
     explainable as a general rule, and the write-up will say what each fix
     addresses.
  3. Compare runs case by case, not just the overall score. A small script lists
     which cases went from fail to pass and from pass to fail, so a fix that
     helps 3 cases but breaks 1 is visible.
  4. Iterate cheaply. Any instruction change re-extracts every case, so I'd
     first test on the 13 failing cases plus about 10 passing ones (roughly 23
     calls), then confirm with one full run.
  5. Check stability. Even at temperature 0 the model isn't perfectly
     repeatable, so run the fixed cases 2–3 times with the cache off. A fix that
     only works sometimes isn't a fix.

  Recommendation

  Start with the low-risk set: 1A, 2A, 3A and 4A, plus regression tests and the
  case-by-case comparison. If quote bugs remain after that, move to 2B (cite by
  line number), which removes quote copying altogether.

  Two decisions for you:
  1. Did you mean these four bugs, or others you've spotted?
  2. Can I commit 7a and 7b first, so chunk 8's changes stay separate and easy
     to review?

  ensures that the AI sourced the info form someone without haveing all the
  issues w quote checks so i think we should move forward with that and then i
  think the rest of them are okay to fail, i think we made good progress with
  the codebase lets see what the new score is with the suggested change, and if
  i didnt say already i approve

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ The new score is 100%, up from 87.33%. The earlier 7a and 7b work is committed
  and pushed; the line-number change is ready for your approval.

  ┌──────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────┬──────────┬───────┐
  │                                  │ Original baseline │ Last run │  Now  │
  ├──────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────┼──────────┼───────┤
  │ Decision matches the label       │ 33/50             │ 44/50    │ 50/50 │
  ├──────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────┼──────────┼───────┤
  │ Issue categories match the label │ 29/50             │ 37/50    │ 50/50 │
  ├──────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────┼──────────┼───────┤
  │ Score                            │ 74.67%            │ 87.33%   │ 100%  │
  └──────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────┴──────────┴───────┘

  - All 13 previously failing cases now pass. None of the 37 that already passed
    broke.
  - There were no errors, no fail-safe triggers, and no facts discarded by the
    check.

  One honest caveat: I re-ran the 13 previously failing cases 3 times each with
  the cache off, and 38 of the 39 runs matched. Case 00048 matched 2 times out
  of 3. In the bad run the model again counted an anesthesia note as an H&P (the
  bug you said was fine to leave). The decision was still right; only one issue
  category was missing. So the expected score is just under 100%, and the
  write-up should say so.

  What changed

  The model now gives a document and line number for each fact, and the code
  copies that line out of the document. Before, the model typed out the quote
  itself.

  - What the model sees: every document line is numbered, like [12] Vitals 10:05
     BP 129/71 mmHg…, and the model answers with doc_id and line: 12. It never
    writes quote text. The quote fields are hidden from its instructions, and a
    test enforces that.
  - The check (verify.py): the cited line must exist and not be blank, and the
    values the model claims must actually appear on that line (BP and
    temperature numbers, the drug name). Then the code copies the line into
    quote, so every quote a nurse sees is the document's exact text.
  - What it fixed:
    - the garbled degree sign (7 cases)
    - quotes stitched together from separate places (3 cases)
    - unexpectedly, the 2 H&P cases and the "will be addressed after surgery"
      case (00020), with no prompt changes. My best guess is that having to
      point at a specific line made the model more careful. In 00020 it now
      cites no after-surgery instruction, so the rule correctly flags the plan
      as incomplete.
  - Removed: the text-comparison code, because quotes are now copied, not
    compared.
  - Added: a small Document.lines() helper, so the numbered text sent to the
    model and the line check split documents the same way.

  How I tested it

  - 187 tests pass. They include a regression test built from the real case
    00003 text containing 98.1 °F.
  - Mutation check: I broke the line logic 7 ways, including off-by-one
    numbering, letting line 0 wrap around to the last line, accepting blank
    lines, and skipping the value check. Each break was caught. My mutation
    script mislabeled one result; running it by hand confirmed the right tests
    fail.
  - Stability: 39 runs with the cache off, described above.

  four ways instead of asserting it:

  1. Is the scorer right? Recompute the score myself from the raw outputs and
     labels, without using run_evals.py.
  2. Did labels leak into the pipeline? Make sure no code that produces answers
     can see the labels.
  3. Is the evidence right, or just the answer? The scorer only checks the
     decision and categories, not whether the cited lines actually support them.
  4. How much of this is fitting to these 50 cases?
