⏺ Seven of the 41 passed H&P checks cite lines, like a planned procedure or a
  date of service, that don't prove the document is an H&P. I'm now checking
  whether those cited documents are the right type with weak evidence, or the
  wrong document entirely.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ All 7 cited documents are genuine pre-op H&Ps. In every case the document's
  first line is the heading PREOPERATIVE HISTORY AND PHYSICAL. So the decisions
  are right, but the model pointed to a weaker line than the obvious one.

  The answer: yes, it's a real 100%, but understand what that number means

  What I confirmed:
  - The score is real. I recomputed it from the raw outputs without the provided
    scorer: 50/50 decisions, 50/50 categories, 100.00%.
  - No cheating. No code that produces answers can see the labels.
    run_baseline.py only passes the submission, and the cache key is built from
    the submission alone.
  - The fixes hold. 12 of the 13 fixed cases passed 3 out of 3 times with the
    cache off.

  What 100% does not mean:

  1. It's measured on the same 50 cases I learned from. Several rules came from
     reading these labelers' explanations, beyond the policy itself:
     - missing temperature → MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA
     - a blank procedure date produces only MISSING_REQUIRED_DATA
     - the newest consent wins
     - 30 days still counts as within 30 days

     I believe they're reasonable readings of the policy, but this is the
     equivalent of a training-set score. New charts with different wording or
     formats would very likely score lower. Without held-out cases, the honest
     statement is "100% on the sample set". It doesn't show how well this
     generalizes.
  2. It isn't fully repeatable. The model varies a little even at temperature 0.
     Case 00048 was right 2 times out of 3, so the expected score is just under
     100%.
  3. The scorer only checks the decision and the issue categories. It never
     checks whether the evidence is good. My audit found 7 H&P checks citing a
     weak line (Planned procedure: …, Date of service: …) instead of the H&P
     heading. The answers are right, but a nurse reading that evidence would
     have to open the document to confirm it's an H&P, which defeats the
     purpose.
  4. 50 cases is small. Each case is 2% of the score, so one flaky case moves it
     noticeably.

  Suggestion

  Issue 3 is cheap to fix. One sentence in the H&P line description: "Cite the
  heading or line that identifies the document as an H&P." It changes the
  extraction instructions, so all 50 cases would be re-extracted (about 50
  calls). I'd re-run the score plus the same evidence audit to confirm the weak
  citations are gone and nothing regressed.

  How do you want to proceed?
  1. Commit the line-number change now, then do the H&P evidence fix as its own
     small commit (my recommendation; it keeps the 100% change and the evidence
     tweak separate for review).
  2. Fold the H&P fix into this commit.
  3. Skip it and list it as a known limitation in the write-up.

  Either way, the write-up will report the score with these four caveats.

      22 -## Key decisions                                               
      15 +**Errors never fail open.** Bad input, an API failure, or a bug
         + returns `NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP` with the reason, never READY and nev
         +er a crash. If the LLM call fails, a structured vital over the

      16  
      24 -1. **LLM extracts, code decides.** Removes the baseline's reaso
         -ning errors, makes every decision traceable to a rule, and make
         -s rules unit-testable without API calls.                       
      25 -2. **Cite line numbers; code copies the quote.** Documents are 
         -sent with numbered lines and the model returns `doc_id` + `line
         -`. Code checks the line exists and that the claimed values (BP,
         - temperature, drug) appear on it, then copies the exact line as
         - the quote. An earlier version had the model copy quotes; it ga
         -rbled characters (`°F`) and stitched fragments together, so val
         -id facts failed verification. Citing lines fixed that and took 
         -the score from 87% to 100%.                                    
      26 -3. **Unverifiable facts are discarded and flagged.** A fact tha
         -t fails verification is dropped and reported as `MISSING_REQUIR
         -ED_DATA`, so it can never help a case reach READY.             
      27 -4. **Fail safe, never fail open.** Invalid input, an API error,
         - or a bug returns `NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP` with the reason; nothing cr
         -ashes or defaults to READY. If extraction fails, rules that nee
         -d the notes are marked "not evaluated", but a structured vital 
         -over the limit still gives `NOT_CLEARED`.                      
      28 -5. **Evidence a nurse can check quickly.** Every issue and ever
         -y passed check lists its sources (document line, lab id, vital,
         - or field) and the computed values (e.g. "31 days before, limit
         - 30"). Medications no rule acted on are listed too, so a review
         -er can see nothing was skipped.                                
      29 -6. **Policy as data.** Windows, thresholds, lab codes, and the 
         -anticoagulant list live in `policy.py`; each rule is a small fu
         -nction in a registry. A policy change is an edit to data, and a
         - new rule is one function plus tests.                          
      30 -7. **Resolve what the policy resolves; flag the rest.** A reche
         -ck replacing an older BP or a newer signed consent replacing a 
         -pending one is resolved in code and shown in the evidence. Disa
         -greements the policy can't settle (e.g. med list says taking, n
         -ote says stopped) become a conflict issue with both sources.   
      31 -8. **Send the model only what it needs.** No patient name, MRN,
         - or DOB. The structured procedure date and med list are also wi
         -thheld, so the model can't echo values its facts are later chec
         -ked against.                                                   
      32 -9. **Repeatable runs.** Temperature 0, `store=False`, and an on
         --disk cache keyed by model, prompt, schema, and input (the Resp
         -onses API has no seed). `make determinism` is disabled because 
         -the cache would make it always report 100%.                    
      33 -10. **No RAG.** Each chart is about 2k tokens, so retrieval wou
         -ld only add a way to miss facts.                               
      17 +**Every result shows its evidence.** Issues and passed checks l
         +ist the exact source (document line, lab, vital, or field) and 
         +the math, like "31 days before, limit 30". Medications no rule 
         +used are listed too, so a nurse can verify a READY case fast.  
      18  
      35 -## Policy interpretations                                      
      19 +**Policy lives in one data file.** Windows, thresholds, lab cod
         +es, and the anticoagulant list are in `policy.py`, and each rul
         +e is a small function in a list. Changing the policy means edit
         +ing data, and adding a rule means adding one function.         
      20  
      37 -- Windows are inclusive: 30 days passes, 31 fails (14/15 for hi
         -gh risk).                                                      
      38 -- Only the most recent result counts. Same-day readings that ca
         -n't be ordered (no times, or UTC vs. note clock) use the most s
         -evere one.                                                     
      39 -- Celsius is converted in code; 38.0 °C = 100.4 °F, which is no
         -t a fever (policy says > 100.4).                               
      40 -- Aspirin and clopidogrel are antiplatelets, not anticoagulants
         -. An anticoagulant mentioned only in a note still counts.      
      41 -- A plan must be for the same drug and say both when to stop an
         -d when to restart; "will be addressed after surgery" is not a r
         -estart instruction.                                            
      42 -- The newest consent wins; it must be signed by the patient and
         - name the scheduled procedure.                                 
      43 -- Missing procedure date, risk, temperature, or BP is `MISSING_
         -REQUIRED_DATA`; a missing date skips the window checks rather t
         -han failing them.                                              
      44 -- Entered-in-error labs are skipped; a BMP does not count as a 
         -CMP. Wellness visits do not count as a pre-op H&P.             
      21 +**Conflicts get resolved by the policy when possible, flagged w
         +hen not.** A recheck replaces an older BP, and a newer signed c
         +onsent replaces a pending one, with both shown as evidence. If 
         +sources truly disagree (med list says taking, note says stopped
         +), it's flagged with both quotes.                              
      22  
      46 -## Known limitations                                           
      23 +**The model only gets what it needs.** No name, MRN, or DOB. It
         + also doesn't see the procedure date or med list, so it can't j
         +ust repeat values we later check its facts against.            
      24  
      48 -- **Small, in-sample evaluation** (above). A held-out set is th
         -e most important next step.                                    
      49 -- **One unstable judgment:** whether a note *mentioning* an H&P
         - is itself an H&P (case 00048, 2/3).                           
      50 -- **Weak evidence lines:** 7 of 41 passing H&P checks cite a li
         -ne like "Planned procedure: …" instead of the H&P heading. The 
         -documents are correct; the evidence is weaker than it should be
         -. A one-line prompt change should fix it.                      
      51 -- **Misspelling tolerance has a floor:** heavily scrambled drug
         - names (e.g. "Xeralto") aren't matched, but they appear in `ign
         -ored_medications` for review.                                  
      52 -- **Runs are sequential;** 50 cases take a few minutes uncached
         -.                                                              
      25 +**Runs are repeatable.** Temperature 0 plus an on-disk cache. `
         +make determinism` is off because the cache would make it always
         + say 100%.                                                     
      26  
      54 -## Process                                                     
      27 +## Policy calls I made                                         
      28  
      56 -Analyzed the baseline's failures, planned the design, then buil
         -t it in small reviewed commits. Each commit had unit tests, a m
         -utation check (deliberately breaking logic to confirm tests cat
         -ch it), and, once wired up, a case-by-case comparison against t
         -he previous run to catch regressions.                          
      29 +- Windows are inclusive (30 days passes, 31 fails).            
      30 +- Latest reading wins; if same-day readings can't be ordered, t
         +he most severe one is used.                                    
      31 +- 38.0 °C = 100.4 °F, which isn't a fever (policy says > 100.4)
         +.                                                              
      32 +- Aspirin and clopidogrel aren't anticoagulants. A blood thinne
         +r mentioned only in a note still counts.                       
      33 +- A plan has to cover both stopping and restarting the same dru
         +g; "will be addressed after surgery" doesn't count.            
      34 +- Newest consent wins, and it must be signed by the patient for
         + the scheduled procedure.

         +ntered-in-error labs are skipped, and a BMP isn't a CMP.       
      36 +

         +instead of the H&P heading. The documents are right, the eviden
